223. JOHN WARD was indicted for stealing, on the 22d of October, 1 brooch, value 12s., the goods of Hannah Harwood.
HANNAH HARWOOD. I live with my father, in East-street, Mary-le-bone - he keeps a ham shop. On Sunday, the 22d of October, between twelve and one o'clock, the prisoner came there, and asked for a plate of beef - I had left my brooch and a locket on a table in the shop a quarter of an hour before; I was in the kitchen when the prisoner first came in - he went out, I missed my brooch, which I have not found.
WILLIAM HARWOOD. The prisoner came into my shop, and asked for half a pound of beef, which he eat there - before he sat down I thought I heard something rattle - I turned my head, and saw my daughter's locket in his hand - he brought it and laid it on the mantel-shelf - he was quite a stranger - after he was gone, I went into the kitchen; my daughter went into the shop, then came and asked if I had seen her brooch - I said, "No, I did not know it was there" - I had locked the shop-door before I left - nobody could have come in.
PHILLIP HARVEY. I was at this shop on the 22d of October, and saw the prisoner there; and on Monday I heard of this loss: and on Tuesday I went there again - the prisoner came in and asked for a plate of soup - I said he was the person who had the brooch. The prosecutrix desired me to follow him, which I did - he went to another shop, and then to East-street, where he borrowed a barrow; I went and brought him back - he acknowledged being there, and having the beef; and afterwards said he had stolen or taken the brooch.
HENRY STOWELL. I am an officer, and took him into custody - he said he had stolen the brooch.
GUILTY. Aged 32.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18261207-221]
224. ELIZABETH WHEATLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of November, 1 yard of silk, value 2s.; 2 yards of calico, value 1s., the goods of John Perkins; and 1 table-cloth, value 1s.; 1 night-gown, value 1s.; 2 spoons, value 4s.; 3 caps, value 1s.; 1 shawl, value 3s.; 1 bonnet, value 3s.; 1 box, value 6d.; 1 umbrella, value 1s.; 1 pair of pattens, value 1s.; 1 shift, value 2s.; 2 shirts, value 4s.; 4 pairs of stockings, value 1s.; 2 towels, value 6d.; 2 handkerchiefs, value 1s.; 1 apron, value 6d.; 1 pair of gloves, value 1s.; 1 dish, value 4d.; 1 basin, value 2d.; and 1 bucket, value 1s., the goods of James Leggett.
JOHN PERKINS. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Church-row, St. Pancrass. On Sunday, the 19th of November, Mrs. Leggett, who lodges with me, came to my room, and asked me to take the prisoner in as a lodger - we agreed - she was to sleep on a sofa-bed in the front parlour. On the, Tuesday evening following, Mr. and Mrs. Leggett went out between eight and nine o'clock. They live in the front kitchen. The prisoner soon afterwards told my wife, in my presence, that she was going out; she went out, and returned in about ten minutes; and then said she was going to have her bonnet cleaned - she went out soon after with a bonnet-box in one hand, and an umbrella in the other - she said she should return; but did not. I afterwards received information from Leggett, and next night the pawnbroker called, and shewed me some silk and other things of mine.
CHARLOTTE PERKINS. I am the wife of John Perkins. This silk is mine; it was locked up in the front-parlour, where the prisoner slept; the key was in my bed-room, which is the back-parlour. There were no marks of violence on the door - it must have been opened by a false key.
JAMES LEGGETT. I lodge at Mr. Perkins'; - the prisoner drank tea with me on the 21st of November. I and my wife went out about nine o'clock that evening. I returned about ten o'clock, and missed a hat-box and the articles stated in the indictment.
ANN LEGGETT. The prisoner came to lodge at the house on the 19th of November; - on the 21st I went out with my husband - I had been away about an hour, and had left the prisoner in the house - we missed this property.
ISAAC KEMPTON. I am an officer, and took the prisoner last Monday. I found a hat-box, umbrella, and a pair of pattens - and on her person I found fifteen duplicates.
JOHN ABITHEL. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Gray's Inn-lane; - on Tuesday, the 21st of November, the prisoner pawned two tea-spoons.
ELIZABETH BARTLETT. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Cromer-street - the prisoner pawned a shawl with me.
JAMES FRANCIS THOMPSON. I am shopman to a pawnbroker; and have a remnant of silk pawned on the 21st of November by the prisoner.
JOHN BRITTON. I am a pawnbroker, and live at Battle-bridge. I have a remnant of calico which was pawned on the 20th of November - I cannot say who by.
JOHN LIMBRICK. I was with Kempton; I have a duplicates of this property.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18261207-222]
225. WILLIAM LEWIS was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of November, 4 drinking glasses, value 4s., the goods Henry Jacobs.
HENRY JACOBS. I am a dealer in glass, and live in Mansel-street, Goodman's-fields; - I had employed the prisoner for a few days, and paid him 10s.; and on the 20th of November he came and asked if I had any thing for him to do - I said No; he then asked me to give him a few glasses to take out as a pattern, as he thought he could sell some. A gentleman came in, whom I went into the parlour with. I came out, and the prisoner asked the price of the glasses, and I told him; and as he was going out, the gentleman said he had robbed me - I called him back - he hesitated about returning - but I collared him - he then opened his coat and took out seven tumblers and a wine-glass, and in his breeches pocket were two wine-glasses, which I missed out of a locker; - he begged for mercy, and said it was his first offence.
JOHN BASNETT. I am a glass-cutter. I saw the prisoner in Jacobs' shop, wiping some glasses - I saw him walk from place to place, and saw him take six tumblers(three in a nest stuck together with hay) from a locker near the floor - Jacobs was then in the parlour; he came into the shop in five minutes, and the prisoner asked the price of some other glasses which he had picked out - Jacobs went into the parlour again, and the prisoner walked to the back of the counter, and fetched a goblet - Jacobs saw him, and asked what he did there; he said he was looking out another goblet, and asked the price of some on the counter - he then asked the weight of two, which was told him; he said he could not believe it, and went and weighed them; at this time I saw him take something out of a nest, and put into his pocket - he walked up, weighed the tumbler, and said it was very good - he took a wine-glass, and put it into his pocket; I heard it knock against the others in his pocket - Jacobs returned into the shop; the prisoner had two weights in his hand; he put them down, and packed up some ale-glasses and goblets which he had looked out on the counter - he was going out, and I said he had something in his pocket; Jacobs called him back and said, "Let me look at those things you have got;" he said, "What things? I have none but what you know of;" Jacobs then said to me, "What was it my son spoke about?" I said, "A gross of wines" - the prisoner had then got into the shop - Jacobs seized him, and found this glass on him; he begged him, for Heaven's sake, to let him go; when the officer came, Jacobs loosened his hold, and he ran out into the Tenter-ground - I pursued, and saw him taken.
Prisoner's Defence. I had bought some glasses at a sale - I went and looked out the pattern glasses: I have the same coat on, and there is no pocket in it; he sent me into the warehouse himself.
HENRY JACOBS. I do not know that he was in the warehouse - the glasses were in the shop.
GUILTY. Aged 42.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18261207-223]
226. CHARLES LESSING was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of August, 1 work-box, value 11s., and 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of Charles Cameron.
MARY WATKINS. I live at Charles Cameron's, a cabinetmaker, in Dorrington-street, Clerkenwell. On the 4th of August, about eleven o'clock in the morning, the prisoner came into the shop, and asked to look at some work-boxes; he chose two, and said they were to be sent to Sadler's Wells, and he would meet the person there, and send one of them back, and the money for the other - I put them into a handkerchief, and Charles Cameron, Jun., took them; his cousin went with him - he returned in an hour with one box, but no money - I am sure of his person; he wanted me once before to send some goods out.
CHARLES CAMERON, JUN. I am the prosecutor's son - I went with the boxes, and when I got near Sadler's Wells, I met the prisoner; he told me to sit down on the steps of a door; to give him one box, and his mother would call on Saturday and pay for it, and bring the handkerchief - I saw him knock at three doors, but he did not go in - I lost sight of him after he knocked at the third door.
ANDREW LLOYD. William Reed (who is not here) apprehended the prisoner - the box has not been found.
CHARLES CAMERON. I missed a work-box from my shop.
Prisoner's Defence. I was coming from Mr. Trueman's to attend a sale, and a person told me to buy a work-box; I went to Cameron's, and took it to the house of the person who wanted it - I left it there, and told the lad that the lady would call in the morning.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18261207-224]
227. THOMAS GOULDEN and GEORGE BETHELL were indicted for stealing, on the 8th of December, 6 lbs. of ham, value 5s., the goods of William Yale.
Bethell pleaded GUILTY. - Whipped and Discharged.
JAMES MEDLEYCOTT. I am a Bow-street patrol. On the 8th of December I met the prisoners about one hundred yards from Mr. Yale's shop; Goulden had this ham in his hands, partly concealed under his coat; Bethell had nothing; Goulden said it belonged to Bethell, who said, after hesitating, that he got it from a shop over the way.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. How was it concealed? A. Under his coat, and wrapped in a cloth.
WILLIAM YALE. This ham is mine; I saw the two prisoners looking through my window, and drove them away about seven o'clock - about nine Medleycott came, and I missed it.
Cross-examined. Q. Was your window down? A. Yes; it was about five feet from the door; I was in the parlour.
GOULDEN's Defence. I met this boy - we went in together - he went over, and returned with this ham, and told me to put it under my coat.
GOULDEN - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18261207-225]
228. ROBERT GEE was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of December, 1 live pig, price 30s., the property of John Smith.
JOHN SMITH. I live in Anchor-hope-alley, and am a baker. On the 1st of December, when I opened my shop, my pig ran out after my boy into the street; I afterwards missed it - I kept it in a shed.
JOHN SMITH. I live next door to the prosecutor. On the 4th of December, the prisoner borrowed a knife and gamble of me, saying he was going to kill a pig - he returned them in the afternoon.
ELIZABETH MASTERS. I live in Love-lane, near Smith's back premises. On the 1st of December I met the prisoner driving a pig from Smith's; I knew it to be Smith's house-pig - it wanted to return - he would not let it; I have seen him about with a dog-cart, and some trotters; I am certain of him.
JAMES WHITE. I took him in charge - Masters said he was the man.
Prisoner's Defence. I borrowed the knife to kill a pig for Mr. Gardener at the Globe public-house.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18261207-226]
229. RACHAEL ELLIS was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of December, 1 tea-board, value 3s., the goods of Margaret Phillips; and 1 sheet, value 2s., the goods of Susannah Weetman.
MARGARET PHILLIPS. I am a widow, and live at Shadwell. The prisoner came to my house on the 2d of December, and slept there two nights - on the 4th of December she said she was going to a situation; on that evening I went out, and on my return she was gone: I missed the tea-board and sheet: the sheet was Weetman's - it was dirty in the cupboard that morning; the tea-board was mine, and hung in the room at tea time.
WILLIAM SALMON. I am a constable. I took up the prisoner on the 6th of December, and found on her duplicates for the sheet and tea-board - the pawnbroker delivered me the tea-board up.
JOHN JOHNSON. I am a pawnbroker. This sheet was pawned with me; I cannot say who by, but I gave the person this duplicate.
SUSANNAH WEETMAN. This sheet is mine - I gave it to Phillips to wash.
GUILTY. Aged 38.
Transported for Seven Years.
There were three other indictments against the prisoner.

230. JAMES CAPES was indicted for stealing, on the the 2d of December, 1 sack, value 2s.; 10 lbs. of hair, value 8s., and 125 bullock and cow-horns, value 4l., the goods of Thomas Green.
WILLIAM MERRY. I am a constable. Last Saturday week, about half-past six o'clock in the evening, I saw the prisoner in Ball-yard, Golden-lane; I stopped him, and asked what he had got in his sack - he said cow-hair, which he got from his master, Mr. Page, and, in consequence of what I heard, I went to Mr. Kember.
JAMES MINGARD. I am a lamp-lighter, and live in Ball-yard. About a month ago I saw the prisoner coming out of a shed there, with a bag or sack on his back; I could not see what was in it - he carried it to his own room; I went, and saw it was a quantity of bullocks' horns - he afterwards told me he had got them from Green's shed, and was going to take them to Mr. Kember's.
WILLIAM KEMBER. I am an ivory comb-maker. I remember buying a few horns of the prisoner - he left a sample one day, the beginning of November, when I was out.
THOMAS GREEN. I deal in horns and hair. I missed some hair last Monday, and the Wednesday before, and at other times, from my shed; it was in two sacks, and both sacks were gone; I believe the hair in this sack to be mine; it is of the same description, and the bag I believe to be mine; I have not found the other - this has the initials of the person I bought the hair of - it was safe about four months ago; I think I saw it three weeks ago - the man I hire the shed of ought to mind it.
GUILTY . Aged 21.
Judgment Respited.
[trialAccount t18261207-228]
231. MARY CROCHETT was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of December, 3 pairs of pattens, value 2s. 6d., the goods of Walter Pitman.
JAMES RICHARDSON. I live in Whitecross-place, and am shopman to Walter Pitman, a shoemaker. Between three and four o'clock, on the 1st of December, I heard the clanking of pattens; I saw the prisoner pass the window - I pursued, and took her with these pattens.
SAMUEL BRIDGES. I took her in charge.
GUILTY. Aged 49.
Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18261207-229]
232. WILLIAM BANBURY was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of December, 3 live tame ducks, price 7s., the property of James Elms.
MATTHEW ELMS. I am the son of James Elms, of Heston. On the 7th of December Giddens came, and said somebody was killing the ducks; I went to the place, and the man was gone; I went in pursuit, and found the prisoner under a hedge, with these ducks tied in a brown handkerchief, he was stooping down; he then ran, and I after him - he jumped into the canal, and there he left the ducks - some women in a tow-boat raked them out - they did not sink; I took the prisoner, and asked how he came to take them - he said he did it through want - he was about a hundred yards from my father's, when I saw him.
JONATHAN GIDDENS. I saw the prisoner in a meadow, near Mr. Elms', at Heston, North-hyde - he was killing the ducks in the meadow; I gave an alarm - Elms' son and I went in pursuit to the canal side, and I heard him flounce into the water - he is the man who took the ducks - he carried them about half-way across the canal in his month, and there left them.
Prisoner's Defence. I saw a man get over the hedge with the ducks - he ran away, and I took them up.
GUILTY . Aged 18.
Judgment Respited.
[trialAccount t18261207-230]
233. THOMAS CALLINDER was indicted for stealing, on the 22d of November, 2 sixpences, 12 penny pieces,15 half-pence, and 1 farthing, the monies of William Allibone, his master.
WILLIAM ALLIBONE. I am a baker. The prisoner was in my service - on the 22d of November, about eleven o'clock in the morning. I went to give change for 1s., and there was only 3 1/2d. in the till; I fetched a constable, who found this money; the prisoner confessed that he took it from the till - his brother desired me to prosecute.
JOHN ALEXANDER COX. I am headborough. The prosecutor told the prisoner if he would say where the money was, he would forgive him, but he would not; I took him to the watch-house, and, about nine o'clock in the evening, he said I should find it under the coals, which I did.
Prisoner. The other boy took it, and gave it to me.
- CALLINDER. I am the prisoner's brother; I told the prosecutor if he got a slight punishment, I did not care if he prosecuted - he told me his other boy had robbed him.
GUILTY. Aged 13.
Whipped and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18261207-231]
234. MARY SULLIVAN and THOMAS BURGESS were indicted for stealing, on the 4th of December, 1 hat, value 2s., the goods of John Henry Hoskyns, from his person.
JOHN HENRY HOSKYNS. I live in Cirencester-place, Fitzroy-square. I was in St. Giles, about twelve o'clock, on the 4th of December - Sullivan came up, forced her arm into mine, and asked me to go home with her, which I declined - we went on till I came to a turning, and there I broke from her - she ran at me, took my hat off, and kicked me violently in the groin; I seized her - her shawl came off; I said, "My good woman, I have no vindictive feeling - give me my hat, and I will give you your shawl"- Burgess then came up, and said if I did not give her the shawl, he would rip my*** open; I have not found it.
WILLIAM CHURCHILL. I saw Burgess with the hat - Sullivan was in a line with him; the prosecutor was there, but I could not hear what was said - Burgess covered the hat with the shawl.
MAURICE LEONARD. I am a watchman. I heard a cry, ran up, and saw the prisoners, who had passed my box together about five minutes before I ran up; I did not see the hat, he was quite sober.
SULLIVAN's Defence. He was in liquor, and wanted to take liberties with me - he pulled my shawl off, and his hat fell off - I saw nothing of it afterwards.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18261207-232]
235. MARY HARRIS and MARGARET KEEF were indicted for stealing, on the 5th of November, 2 half-crowns, 8 shillings, and 1 sixpence, the monies of Paul Hall, from his person.
The prosecutor did not appear. NOT GUILTY.

236. ELIZABETH BURGIN was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of November, 1 watch, value 5l.; 1 seal, value 12s.; 1 key, value 3s., and 1 chain, value 3d., the goods of Edward Bell, from his person.
EDWARD BELL. I am master of the York packet. On the 12th of November I was at the Coach and Horses public-house, Shadwell, and saw the prisoner there about seven o'clock in the morning - I spoke to her, but went no where with her, on my oath.
Q. Look at this; is it your writing? A. Yes; (looking at his deposition) I signed this; but on my oath I went no where with her - she took my watch from me in the street - I do not know in what street - it was a quarter of a mile from the public-house; she had walked after me, but not with me; she took my watch in an alley, and I ran after her - it was produced before the Magistrate.
Prisoner. Q. Did you not take me to the Coach and Horses, and give me a glass of spirits? A. I believe I did treat her - I received the watch-chain from the landlord of the Coach and Horses.
GEORGY DEVERELL. I received this watch from Ayer on the Monday; there was no seal or key to it; she said she had given the girl 2s. for it, and it belonged to her husband.
MARGARET AYER. I am the wife of Henry Ayer. On the 12th of November the prisoner brought a watch to me, which I gave to Deverell - she owed me 3s. 6d. for victuals - I would not trust her any more, and she said she would leave this watch - that it was her husband's, who was dead - she had a man with her - they both dined at my house.
BASIL SANDS. I am a waiter at the Coach and Horses - I found a watch, chain, seal, and key, under a seat where the prisoner had sat with another woman and boy, on the 12th of November, about four o'clock; I saw the prosecutor with her, between seven and eight o'clock that morning; he was not sober; he had been in the house the night before; my master took his watch, and returned it to him in the morning; the prisoner came in at three o'clock with another woman and a boy, and had a pint of beer; she told my master she was charged with stealing the watch.
The prisoner put in a written defence, stating that the prosecutor had spent the evening with her, and given her the watch.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18261207-234]
237. ELIZABETH HOLLAND was indicted for stealing, on the 2d of December, 2 sheets, value 6s.; a counterpane, value 4s.; 1 looking-glass, value 2s., and 1 flat-iron, value 1s., the goods of George Read, in a lodging room.
MATILDA READ. I am the wife of George Read, who lives in Orchard-street, Westminster. On the 22d of November I let the prisoner a furnished room. She left on the 2d of December without notice; I went up that day for my rent; as she was not there, I got a constable to break the door open; I missed an iron, a looking-glass, a counterpane, and a pair of sheets, which were let with the room.
LEONARD NEED. I am a pawnbroker. The prisoner pawned this sheet with me on the 27th of November.
BENJAMIN TIMBRELL. I apprehended the prisoner on the 2d of December, and found a duplicate of this sheet on her.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. All she says is false, except about the sheets, which I intended to get back: I was in distress.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18261207-235]
283. HENRY MARLTON was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of December, 169 lbs. weight of lead, value 20s., thegoods of George Watkins, the younger, and fixed to a building of his.
TIMOTHY COLTMAN. I am a watchman at Mr. Watkins' premises, Henry-street, Lambeth. On the 8th of December, at six o'clock in the evening, I was alarmed by one of the workmen; we got on the top of the house with a ladder, and found the prisoner there, with this roll of lead by his side; it appeared fresh cut; he had two knives; I did not know him before; it is an unfinished house.
GEORGE WATKINS. I belong to this house - this lead was on the roof - the prisoner is a stranger.
THOMAS WOOD. I am headborough. I received the prisoner in charge with the lead; I found two knives and two keys on him; the lead fits, what is left on the house, exactly.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I heard an alarm, and got into the house with others.
TIMOTHY COLTMAN. There was nobody but him there; he was laying in the gutter.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.

239. WILLIAM GIBBS was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of November, 1 pair of boots, value 1l., the goods of David Davis; and 1 hammer, value 1s.; 1 knife, value 4d.; 1 rasp, value 1d.; 1 stamp, value 6d.; a pair of pincers, value 1d.; and a brush, value 1d., the goods of William Rushton.
ANN DAVIS. I am the wife of David Davis, and worked with Mr. Rushton, in New Tothill-street - the prisoner also worked with me as a shoemaker; I gave him some boots and shoes to sell on the 6th of November - he took these boots with him, and never returned.
JOHN SIMPSON. I am an officer. I was sent for on the 10th of November; I found the prisoner with Rushton's property, in Lilley-street, Saffron-hill.
WILLIAM RUSHTON. I am in the 1st regiment of Guards; I had these tools at Davis's; - we at times borrow each other's tools.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18261207-237]
240. SOPHIA ZEALEY was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of November, 1 coat, value 8s., and 1 pair of trousers, value 2s. the goods of John Marchant.
JOHN MARCHANT. I live in Dartmouth-street, Westminster. The prisoner lodged in my house on the 6th of November. I missed my coat and trousers from my bed-room; the prisoner was at the office on some other charge; and she directed us to the pawnbroker; her husband is a butcher.
JOHN LANGLEY. I am an officer. I was taking the prisoner to Tothill-fields. I made her no promise or threat; but she told me this property was pawned at Stocks and Co.'s; I went there next day with Marchant, and found them.
LEONARD GEORGE NEEDS. I live with Stocks and Co. Tothill-street, Westminster. I have a coat and trousers which were pawned by a woman, on the 6th of November, in the name of Maria Watts.
Prisoner's Defence. I said I thought they were there. He said he would give me 2s. to tell him where they were; I saw a person coming out of the room with them, but I did not take them.
JOHN LANGLEY. It is false. She told me she had pawned them, but had lost the duplicate.
GUILTY. Aged 30.
Transported for Seven Years.
There was another Indictment against the Prisoner.

241. JAMES WOODWARD was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of December, 1 stove, value 16s., the goods of Edward Hill Friend.
JOHN GANT. I am servant to Edward Hill Friend, a tinman, of Seven Dials. This stove stood at his door on the 9th of December, about 12 o'clock at night. I saw the prisoner at the window a quarter of an hour before it was taken: - he went on - then returned, and took it; - my master ran after him. I knew him before.
EDWARD HILL FRIEND. I saw the prisoner take the stove, and secured him with it.
THOMAS SPEED. I am a watchman, and took him in charge.
Prisoner's Defence. I did not know it belonged to any body.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Recommended to Mercy.
Confined One Month and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18261207-239]
242. SAMUEL YELDHAN was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of December, 3 sheets, value 12s.; 9 shirts, value 2l.; 4 table cloths, value 2l.; 8 napkins, value 8s.; 6 handkerchiefs, value 12s.; 11 cravats, value 11s.; 1 pillow-case, value 2d.; 2 pairs of stockings, value 2s.; a pair of drawers, value 1s.; 1 shirt, value 1s.; 1 waistcoat, value 1s.; 4 towels, value 4d., and 2 night-caps, value 2d., the goods of James Wood.
JAMES WOOD. I am a coachman, and live at Lisson-grove. I had two or three bundles, one of which contained this property, in a cart; it could not have fallen out - I was in Upper York-street about half-past five o'clock; a woman said there was a bundle gone; I turned and saw a person running with it in his arms about ten yards off; I jumped out of the cart, called Stop thief! and he threw it down; the prisoner was taken in two or three minutes - I believe he is the person - he begged I would forgive him, for the sake of his mother and family; he said he would give me half a sovereign to let him go.
Cross-examined by Mr. BARRY. Q. Were these things for your wife to wash? A. Yes - it was getting dark.
WALTER COTTON. I saw the prisoner running - a butcher caught at him, but did not secure him - I ran and took him; he said, "For God's sake, Mr. Cotton, do not stop me, I have got nothing;" three or four persons came up, and said he had robbed a cart - I detained him; his family are respectable; I am sorry to see him here, for he bore a very good character.
MARY WHEELER. I saw a young man with a bundle - he threw it down on a cry of Stop thief! being raised.
ELIZABETH HEWIT. I saw the bundle taken, but am not certain of the prisoner.
Prisoner's Defence. I saw a man take the bundle; before I touched it a man took it up, and as a chaise was coming, I ran.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Recommended to Mercy by the Jury.
Confined Six Months, and Whipped.
[trialAccount t18261207-240]
Second London Jury - Before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
243. EDWARD CLARKE and GEORGE GRAVES were indicted for feloniously assaulting William Scott, on the 4th of November, putting him in fear, and taking from his person, and against his will, 1 hat, value 1s.; 1 handkerchief, value 6d.; 1 shilling, and three halfpence, his property.
WILLIAM SCOTT. I am a labourer. About twelve o'clock at night, on the 4th of November, I was in Bishopsgate-street, near Sun-street, going home to Angel-alley - I had been drinking, but was not so drunk as not to know what I was about; the prisoners came up, and Clarke asked if I would give them something to drink - I knew them before by sight - I have drank with Clarke; he said, "Scott, give us some gin;" I said I had no money to spare - they both seemed much intoxicated; I walked on, they followed me, and just within a few yards of my house, Clarke put his foot before me, and I fell down on my face; Graves put his hand into my right-hand pocket, and took out one shilling and three halfpence - he asked Clarke if he had got a knife; I was on my face, but I think it was his voice; Clarke said, No; I sung out for the watchman - they went away with my hat and handkerchief; I got up at seven o'clock in the morning, and gave information at a wine-vaults.
RICHARD MORRIS. I am an officer, about seven o'clock in the morning I went into the Black Raven public-house, and found Scott, who told me of his loss; I found the prisoners in bed at a house in Angel-alley; when Clarke put on his clothes they were muddy. I said he had been in the mud: - he said he was so drunk he did not know where he had been. Graves said the same.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18261207-241]
244. GEORGE MASON and JAMES COOK were indicted for breaking and entering the dwelling-house of George Deane, the younger, about four o'clock in the afternoon, on the 13th of November (he and others being therein), and stealing 3 metal boxes, value 4s., the goods of George Deane and George Deane, the younger.
WILLIAM TURNER. I am keeper of the Monument. On the 13th of November, about four o'clock in the afternoon, I saw the two prisoners in company for some time - they at last went up to Mr. Deane's window, at the corner of Monument-yard; I saw Cook remove part of a pane of glass, and take it out; Mason stood at his side; they watched for a few minutes, and then I saw Mason put his hand in, and take three boxes out - they went up Fish-street-hill together; I went and informed Mr. Deane, then pursued up Cannon-street and took them, running in Gracechurch-street, and brought them back - Mason had the boxes.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Did you always say that Cook cut the glass? A. Yes - I do not know what he did it with.
GEORGE DEANE, JUN. This is my dwelling-house - it belongs to the firm; I am in partnership with my father; he was not at home; these boxes are ours, and were near a pane of glass, which had been mended that morning - it was quite secure twenty minutes before; they must have cut it, I think - they are worth 4s.
MASON's Defence. I was going home when he took me.
Several witnesses gave the prisoners a good character.
MASON - GUILTY. Aged 13.
COOK - GUILTY. Aged 15.
Of stealing only. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18261207-242]
245. ROBERT ROSAMOND was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of October, 1 watch, value 36s.; 1 seal, value 4s., and 1 string, value 1d., the goods of William Bowie, from his person.
WILLIAM BOWIE. I am employed at the East-India Company's warehouse, and live in Rudduck's-buildings, Shoreditch. On Saturday, the 28th of October, about six o'clock in the evening, I was in Long-lane, Smithfield, and was stopped by three or four persons, which I at first considered accidental - one of them attempted to take the wall, and as I gave way for him, I was pushed forward, and my watch very forcibly snatched from my fob - I was hustled a little; the one who took it ran down Three-fox-court, to a dark place; I did not sufficiently notice him so as to swear to him - I found the prisoner in custody, with my watch, next night; I ran down the alley, but could not see the person.
EZRA GARDINER. I live at the corner of Three-fox-court, in Long-lane. I was in my shop, and saw three men hustling a man at the corner of my door - I and my brother rushed out; my brother and the prosecutor followed the prisoner in the direction they ran - I had seen three of them run down there; I observed one of them, and firmly believe him to be the prisoner; I went down Long-lane to the pig-market, to meet them at the other end of the court, but before I got there I met the prisoner, who was the first person running; in fact, I saw no other person running - I cannot say whether he came out of the court, as I was then about twenty yards from it. I seized him, and said, "You are the person who robbed the man of his watch" - he said, "You are mistaken, I have done nothing;" my belief is that he is the man.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Was it not dark? A. Yes; the gas was lighted.
WILLIAM FAULKNER. I was coming up Long-lane, at a few minutes after six o'clock, and saw Gardiner and the prisoner scuffling in the road; they got on to the pavement; the prisoner tried to get away, but could not - I saw him take something from his coat-pocket, and throw it into the passage of a house there - I called to the landlord, and said, whatever the man had got, he had thrown into the passage; he called for a light, and this watch and case were picked up in his passage, in two different places.
JOHN DELL. I live at a shop, No. 62, Long-lane - Faulkner called out to me, that the prisoner had thrown something into my passage - I was giving a girl change, and saw the prisoner at the door in custody - I called for a light, and found the watch and case.
JOHN GODFREY. I took him in charge.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I came from Charterhouse-lane into the pig-market, but never touched the watch.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18261207-243]

NOT GUILTY.

247. THOMAS WILSON was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of November, 1 printed book, value 2s., the goods of John Morris.
JOHN MORRIS. I am a bookseller, and live in Bishopsgate-street. On the 7th of November, I saw the prisoner take a book from my stall, and walk off with it in his pocket - I took him about six yards off.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 23.

[trialAccount t18261207-245]
248. JAMES MARSHALL was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of November, 1 watch-chain, value 4l.; 2 seals, value 2l.; 1 key, value 10s., and 1 ring, value 10s., the goods of Thomas Murray, from his person.
THOMAS MURRAY. I am a dealer in marine-stores. On the 5th of November, about half-past six o'clock in the evening, I was going down Cow-lane into Holborn, with Mary White - we were seized by three young men; the prisoner was the one on my left hand - one of them ran against me, and seized my seals; the chain broke, and he got that and the seals - I said, "My chain and seals are gone!" the prisoner at this time was holding my left side; I turned round, and saw the man who seized my chain run down West-street. I said to the prisoner, "I'll have you; you are one of them" - he was with the other two; he tried to get away, but I held him; he dragged me to the top of West-street and knocked my hat off - I then knocked his hat off, and let him go to recover mine.
Cross-examined. Q. I believe he stopped when his hat fell off, and asked you for it? A. No, he did not - I was sure of him before the Alderman - when I called Watch! I was surrounded by a number of people.
MARY WHITE. I was with Murray on Sunday evening; three persons came round us - I do not know who took the chain - they were all in company - Murray took the prisoner; I saw the chain and seals go.
Cross-examined. Q. Are you his sister? A. No; I do not know that he took them.
WILLIAM BUSHNELL. I am a patrol. I heard a call of Watch! I ran up, and saw the prisoner standing by the wall, talking to Murray, who said he had been robbed, and the prisoner was one who was with them.
Cross-examined. Q. How were they standing? A. Within a yard of one another; they were not holding each other at that time - the prisoner said he had knocked his hat off - there was a crowd round.
GEORGE THOMAS HANNINGTON. I received him in charge, but found nothing on him.
Prisoner's Defence. I never had hold of him at all.
THOMAS MURRAY. He held my arm down while the others robbed me - he would have run off Smithfield way, but he wanted to get down West-street, after his companions.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18261207-246]
249. CHARLES MUNDAY and THOMAS DAVIS were indicted for stealing, on the 9th of November, 1 handkerchief, value 6d., the goods of a person unknown, from his person.
JOHN McDONALD. I am a constable. On the 9th of November, about three o'clock in the afternoon, I was in Bridge-street, Blackfriars, and saw a gentleman on the pavement, looking at the Lord Mayor's carriage - I was with the carriage, and saw the prisoners walking along, and trying several persons' pockets - they then came to this gentleman, who stood on the pavement, and drew out a silk handkerchief - I believe Munday drew it out, but Davis was close to him - they saw me, rolled it up, and dropped it - I took it up, and asked the gentleman if he had lost his handkerchief - he felt in his pocket, and said Yes, and claimed it. I took one of the prisoners, and Charles took the other. The gentleman went with us to the Compter, and then said he would not prosecute, and would not give his name.
JOSEPH CHARLES. I am an extra constable; I saw Munday take the handkerchief, and drop it - McDonald picked it up, and we secured the prisoners.
DAVIS's Defence. Some other boy took it up.
MUNDAY - GUILTY. Aged 16.
DAVIS - GUILTY. Aged 16.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18261207-247]
250. EDWARD SEACOMB was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of December, 4 1/2lbs. of mutton, value 2s. 7d.; and 4 1/2lbs. of beef, value 1s. 3d., the goods of Savell Sheppeard.
SAVELL SHEPPEARD. I am a butcher, and was out when this happened. I found the beef at Guildhall.
GEORGE SELWOOD. I am a patrol. I watched the prisoner and two other boys at three shops; when they came to the prosecutor's, the prisoner crossed over, went in, and took this beef; he put the hook down; then crossed the shop, and took a piece of mutton, and came out with both under his coat - he gave a whistle - I followed them down Fan-street; I secured him - Sheppeard claimed the meat - I found 1d. on him.
The prisoner pleaded distress.
GUILTY. Aged 13.
Whipped and Discharged.

251. THOMAS EVANS was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of December, 12 lbs. of pork, value 6s., the goods of James Swain.
JAMES SWAIN. I am a butcher in Newgate Market. On the 1st of December, the prisoner came into my shop, and bought a pair of fore-quarters of mutton; he laid it on the block to chop it; I saw him lay down the chopper, and take up a piece of pork; and then he took another piece up -he placed them on the mutton, and in his tray. I followed him out, and took him; he had paid for the mutton, but had also got 12lbs. of pork. He is a butcher.
THOMAS TAYLOR. I am a salesman. I was in the market; Swain came to me, and said the prisoner had some pork of mine, which was in his shop.
JAMES GIBBON. I received the prisoner in charge.

[trialAccount t18261207-249]
252. JOB CLAY was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of April, 1 truck, value 3l. 10s., the goods of William Hebborn.
GEORGE HALES. I am a labourer at Botolph wharf; the prisoner is a labourer; Mr. Hebborn's truck stood down the gateway - I was standing there on the 1st of April; the prisoner came and said he came for McCree's truck; Hebborn is a porter, and had lived with McCree; I asked if he was sure McCree had sent him for it; he said he did, as he had a quantity of baggage to bring down; he took it, and went towards the Custom-house with it - I saw no more of him.
WILLIAM HEBBORN. I am a porter. This is my truck. I never sent the prisoner for it, or authorised him to get possession of it. I have not seen it since. I took him on the 17th of November. He said he had taken it away, but never said what had become of it. I could not find him before.
Prisoner's Defence. A man came and asked if I wanted a job; he took me up the wharf, and I assisted him with the truck. - Another man joined us, and they took me to Bow, and at last both went away; - they returned, and began swearing, and saying it was not worth the trouble; they could get but 12s. for it.
GUILTY. Aged 53.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18261207-250]
253. JOHN GLOVER was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of December, 1 shoe, value 5s. the goods of George Goodeve.
EUPHEMIA GOODEVE. I am the wife of George Goodeve, a shoemaker; we live in Fenchurch-street. The prisoner came in on the 5th of December and offered some saw-dust for sale; I said we wanted none; he still stood there; I told him to go along, and at last he went: - he had been standing with his back to the window in the shop - he returned in a few minutes - stood in the same place, and told me the price of the saw-dust; I told him to go along; he was turning round, and I saw this shoe in his hand, under a sack, which he had on his shoulder: I called a gentleman in, and he was secured; he dropped it, and the gentleman brought it in. - The fellow one was taken the first time he came, I suppose, as both were there before he came.
JOHN JUDD. - I am an officer, and have the shoe.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18261207-251]
254. RAYMOND DRAKE was indicted for embezzlement.
WILLIAM HUMPHREYS. I am a green-grocer, and live in St. Dunstan's-hill. The prisoner was my servant, and intrusted to receive money for me; he had 13s. a week, and his breakfast; he should account to me directly he received money; Reynolds bought coals of me - the prisoner never accounted to me for money received from him on the 30th of October.
RICHARD REYNOLDS. I am a broker. On the 30th of October, between three and four o'clock, I paid the prisoner, 3s. 2d. for two bushels of coals - I am sure there was one shilling among the money I gave him; he gave me this receipt. (read.)
WILLIAM HUMPHREYS. I did not ask him for this money; he was discharged on the 4th of November, and on the 6th I received information; he called on me - I said I had learned that he had received some money, and not accounted for it, and said I should be obliged if he would tell me if he had received any more; he said he had received this of Mr. Reynolds.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18261207-252]
255. RAYMOND DRAKE was again indicted for the like offence.
WILLIAM HUMPHREYS. The prisoner never paid me any money received from Elizabeth Read on the 30th of October; he took out goods, and if he was paid for them, he should bring me the money directly.
Cross-examined by Mr. BARRY. Q. You have a good business I suppose? A. Yes, and keep books; I discovered this account before the prisoner called; he acknowledged receiving it; he had to pay me several small sums in the course of the day.
ELIZABETH READ. I deal with Humphreys. The prisoner brought coals and potatoes to my house; I paid the prisoner four separate shillings on the 30th of October, for his master; he gave me no receipt.
Cross-examined. Q. How do you recollect the day? A. I remember it very well.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Confined Six Months.

258. THOMAS PERKINS was indicted for stealing, on the 23d of November, 1 piece of leaden pipe, value 3s., and 1 brass-cock, fixed to the same, value 2s., the goods of James Harding, the elder, and James Harding, the younger, and fixed to a certain building of theirs; and 3 chisels, value 1s., the goods of George Crowhurst .
2d COUNT, stating the pipe to belong to James Harding, the elder, only.
JOSEPH RIESHA. I am a watchman of Coleman-street. I went on duty at seven o'clock on the 23d of November, and at the end of Finsbury-circus and London-wall, I saw the prisoner coming over some boards - I laid hold of him by his wooden-leg, and said, "You must go to the watch-house" - I searched him, and found this pipe and cock on him.
WILLIAM PAGE. I am an officer. I found this property on the prisoner at the watch-house - I compared it with what remains at the building - it fits exactly - it is a water-pipe, and was fixed to the cistern.
GEORGE CROWHURST. These three chisels are mine, and were on Messrs. Harding's premises.
JAMES HARDING, JUN. This building was in the Circus, facing the London Institution - it is in the City - it belongs to my father only - I am in partnership with him, but not in this building; I have seen the pipe fitted with what remains - it fits exactly.
GUILTY. Aged 28.
Transported for Seven Years.

259. CHARLOTTE EVERSFIELD was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of November, 1 half-crown, and 4 shillings, the monies of Thomas Alexander Forbes, her master.
THOMAS HATFIELD. I am in the employ of Mr. Forbes, a butcher of Newgate-market - the prisoner was about six weeks in his service. On the 4th of November, between five and six o'clock in the evening, I was minding the shop, outside, and saw her come up the passage, and go behind the door - I went near her, and saw her hand in the till, taking out some money; she took her hand out- I turned round, and saw her go down into the kitchen - I do not know what the till contained; I told my master; who got an officer.
THOMAS ALEXANDER FORBES. Hatfield gave me information - I fetched an officer, who searched her - I do not know what the till contained.
LEWIS FACHE. I am an officer. I asked the prisoner if she had any money in her pocket; she said, No; I found on her half-crown, four shillings, and 5 1/2d.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18261207-257]
260. JOHN HUMPHRIES was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of November, 4 stone-bottles, value 6d., the goods of Thomas James Wallington.
JOHN MORELAND. I am a patrol of St. Sepulchre's. On the 19th of November, at a quarter-past five o'clock, I saw the prisoner close to Mr. Wallington's premises; I was about thirty yards off - he advanced till he was within about fifteen yards of me - I went and collared him with these four stone-bottles under his arm - he said he had picked them up on the steps of the door.
THOMAS JAMES WALLINGTON. About five o'clock in the evening, this occurred - I knew one of these bottles, for I had marked it, having lost ten or twelve dozens on the Thursday evening before; this was taken on Sunday; my cellar had been fastened up; the bars were removed- a person could get his head in and reach them.
Prisoner's Defence. I found them on the steps of the door.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18261207-258]
261. GEORGE WILLIAMS was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of November, 3lbs. weight of tobacco, value 10s., the goods of James Taddy, and others.
WILLIAM HIETT. I am in the employ of James Taddy, and others, his partners, tobacconists, of No. 45, Minories - the prisoner was employed by a man, who works on their premises by the piece; it is my duty to examine them when they leave work. On the 14th of November, about ten minutes to seven o'clock in the evening, the prisoner was leaving work - I searched him at the entrance of the passage, and found in his hat, which he had over his shoulder, a quantity of leaf-tobacco; it was three parts full- there were about 3lbs. of it - I gave information, and he was taken in charge - he had been there three months - I know nothing against him.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Is it not your habit to search all the boys? A. Yes; he did not tell me he had picked it up, intending to place it on a cask; it had a handkerchief over it - he must have took the handkerchief out to have his hat searched - he knew that - I saw no basin or plate in his hand.
COURT. Q. Is it your usual way to rub them down? A. Yes; and then they pull off their hats.
JOHN FORRESTER. I am an officer, and took him with the hat and tobacco.
JAMES TOMLIN. I am one of this firm. It is Hiett's duty to search the men and boys; the prisoner has worked for us about nine months in all.
Prisoner's Defence. I was coming out of the room and picked it up - I robbed nobody.
One witness gave the prisoner a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18261207-259]
262. PATRICK MEREDITH was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of November, 16 lbs. of beef, value 8s., the goods of William Gilbert.
WILLIAM GILBERT. I am a butcher, and live in Long-lane, West-Smithfield. On the 11th of November I was writing at the back of the shop - somebody on the pavement asked if I had not missed something; I looked, and missed a piece of sirloin of beef; I was told the man had gone up Long-lane; I and the patrol followed, and caught the prisoner with this beef under his arm; I knew it to be mine.
JOHN MORELAND. I am a patrol. I came up to the corner of Charter-house-street - a lad directed me up Long-lane; I went up, and took the prisoner with the beef, which Mr. Gilbert claimed.
GEORGE GODFREY. I received him in charge at the watch-house, and found some sham gold seals on him - he is a duffer, and sells these articles - he seemed a little drunk when he was taken, but, before we searched him, he got excessively drunk without more drink.
GUILTY. Aged 23.
Publicly Whipped and Discharged.

263. JOHN GOODMAN and ANTHONY FORMAN were indicted for stealing, on the 9th of November, 1 handkerchief, value 6d., the goods of Henry Frazier, from his person.
FRANCIS KEYS. I am an officer. On the 9th of November I was at the end of Blackfriars-bridge, at the time the Lord Mayor was on the water; I saw the prisoners together, following a lady and gentleman; Forman took the handkerchief from a gentleman's pocket, and gave it to Goodman; I crossed over, and told the gentleman - he caught Goodman, and snatched the handkerchief out of his hand; he said his name was Frazier, but did not appear at the office; he gave me no Christian name, but it was marked H.F.

264. JOHN TOWNS and WILLIAM HUNT were indicted for stealing, on the 24th of November, 90 lbs. of lead, value 22s., belonging to David Evans, and fixed to his dwelling-house.
SECOND COUNT, stating it to be fixed to a building.
WILLIAM THRESH. On the 24th of November, about twenty minutes to six o'clock, I was just by Mr. Evans' premises, at the corner of John-street, Crutched-friars, and met Towns with this piece of lead on his shoulder - he was carrying it openly, and I asked where he was going - he said to take it home to his master; I said it was useless to say that; but to take it back to where he got it from; I had seen him ten minutes or a quarter of an hour before, and asked who he was waiting for; he said for some persons who were at work there; I have since compared the lead with what remains on the premises - it corresponds exactly.
JOHN THOMPSON. I am a constable. I met Hunt on the 24th of November, a little before six o'clock in the evening, about a quarter of a mile from Mr. Evans' premises, with this piece of lead in this bag; I asked what he had got - he said, "Some wet things;" I felt it was a substance, and found this lead in it; I have compared it with what remains - it corresponds exactly.
DAVID EVANS. This lead was taken from my manufactory, which adjoins my dwelling-house - it belongs to me; I have seen the lead compared with what remains - they correspond exactly.
JOSEPH HUNT. I am an officer. I took Towns to the Compter; I asked if he had a knife about him; he said No, but I was untying his hands in the Compter, and this knife was in his hands - it appears to have lately cut lead.
TOWN's Defence. I was hired to carry the lead.
TOWNS - GUILTY. Aged 21.
HUNT - GUILTY. Aged 30.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18261207-262]
265. JAMES RAVEN was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of September, 2 sovereigns, 6 half-sovereigns, one 10l., and five 1l. promissory notes, the property of James Wesson.
JAMES WESSON. I am a tailor, and live at Saffron-Walden, Essex. On the 26th of September I packed up a parcel, containing a 10l. and 5l. Bank note of Saffron-Walden bank, with six half-sovereigns, and two sovereigns- it was all mine; I packed it in brown paper, and directed it to Mr. William Aldington Seal, Minories - it was to go to the Blue Boar public-house, Whitechapel - this is the 10l. note. - I gave the parcel to Spinks to deliver to the Stortford coach; I know nothing of the prisoner.
JOHN SPINKS. I drive the coach as far as Stortford. I received this brown paper parcel from Wesson on the 26th of September; I locked it in the hind boot - we change coaches at Bishop-Stortford; I took all the parcels out of my coach, and put them into Firman's.
GEORGE FIRMAN. I drive the Bishop-Stortford coach to town; I did not see this parcel.
JOHN PORTER. I am porter at the Blue Boar, Whitechapel. The Bishop-Stortford coach arrived there, about twelve o'clock in the morning, on the 26th of September - the prisoner was horse-keeper there, and helped to unload the coach; I did not help to unload it that day; I know no more of it.
JOHN FORRESTER. About three weeks after this parcel was taken, I heard the Regulator coach had been robbed of a parcel containing 20l., and, on inquiry, I found the prisoner had left the Blue Boar, and gone to Mrs. Nelson's, at the Bull inn; I went to him, and said, "Young man, I hear you have found a little money;" he said, "Yes, I have;" I said, "How much might it be, pray?" he said a 10l. note; I said, "Where did you find it?" he said, "In Cheapside;" I said, "Was it loose in the street?" he said,"No - it was wrapped in brown paper;" I asked if there was any more; he said, "No - only a 10l. note;" I asked if it was a Bank note - he said, "No - a country note;" I asked where he changed it; he said at Mr. Meason's, the hatter, in Aldgate, and that he gave his address, James Thompson, Leadenhall-street, on the back of it; I went to Mr. Meason, and found this note with that written on it, and here is the hat which he bought; I found thirteen sovereigns in his box.
JOHN STAPLES. I am in the employ of Mr. Meason, hatter, of Aldgate. I remember the prisoner coming to our house once, previous to buying this hat, which was on the 13th or 14th of October - he paid me a 10l. Saffron-Walden note; I rather doubted its being good, and sent to the Saracen's Head inn, where the Saffron-Walden coach comes, to know if it was good, or if it had been stopped - they did not doubt it - I asked him his address - he said James Thompson, Leadenhall-street, and said he worked at Messrs. Cross and Lewis, Fenchurch-street; I paid the note away in the course of the week, and it got down to Saffron-Walden.
JAMES WESSON. This is my note - it came from the Saffron-Walden bank to me; I know it by the number, which I took when I packed it up.
WILLIAM ALDINGTON SEAL. I live at Sparrow-corner Minories. I expected this parcel - it never arrived, and I wrote in consequence of it.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18261207-263]
266. MARIA FILLINGHAM was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of October, 1 silk handkerchief, value 3s., and 1 pencil-case, value 1s., the goods of Thomas Fitch, her master; and 1 handkerchief, value 3s.; 1 petticoat, value 3s.; 1 shift, value 3s., and 2 caps, value 2s., the goods of Mary Ann Osborne.
THOMAS FITCH. I live in St. Dunstan's-passage, City. Mary Ann Osborne is my wife's sister - the prisoner nurse d my wife, who was laying in - a band of ribbon was seen hanging out of her pocket; she was suspected, and taken into custody; and on her person was found a piece of lace, all my wife's keys, and other articles and duplicates.
THOMAS EMBERSON. I live with Mr. Allers, a pawnbrober. I have several articles pawned by the prisoner in the name of Carter.
RICHARD MORRIS. I live with Mr. Thomas, a pawnbroker. I have a pencil-case, a shift, a pair of stockings, and cap, pawned, on the 14th of October, by a woman in the name of E. Carter - I cannot say whether it was the prisoner.
MARY ANN OSBORNE. This property is all mine, except the pencil-case and handkerchief; I never gave her leave to take them.
JOHN THOMPSON. I am an officer. I found the duplicates of this property on the prisoner.
Prisoner's Defence. I intended to redeem them again, and had no idea of defrauding them.
GUILTY. Aged 46.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18261207-264]
267. JOHN BEVERLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of November, 1 pair of goloshes, value 11s., the goods of Thomas Clarke.
THOMAS CLARKE. I am a boot-maker, and live in Fleet-street. About eight o'clock in the evening of the 25th of November, my attention was drawn from the end of the shop, by the head of a person near my window; the prisoner came into the shop, and took this pair of goloshes; I called to my young man - we followed, and took him a few yards off, with them in his hand.
JOSEPH ALVIN. I am apprentice to Mr. Clarke. He called me; I saw the prisoner going out with the goloshes; we followed, and took him with them. He is a stranger.
GUILTY. Aged 33.
Transported Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18261207-265]
268. ROBERT BENNETT was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of November, 1 pair of stockings, value 1s., the goods of Robert Romanis.
JOHN MOODY. I am shopman to Robert Romanis, a hosier, of Cheapside. On the 18th of November, about eight o'clock at night, I was coming into the shop, and saw the prisoner snatch these stockings from inside the door, and run down Friday-street; I followed, but could not overtake him - he turned down Distaff-lane - I lost sight of him for a few seconds - he then turned back, and asked what I wanted with him - he had nothing then - I called the watchman, who took him; I went down Distaff-lane, and found these stockings in a grating.
WILLIAM DANCE. I am a watchman. I saw the prisoner run down Friday-street and turn into Distaff-lane. Moody called to me to take him, and we met him coming back towards Friday-street - I took him to the watch-house; and on going along, he said, "This will only be a three weeks' spell for me again."
EDWARD SORRELL. I am watch-house keeper. The prisoner was brought there with the stockings.
MR. ROBERT ROMANIS. These are my stockings; they were inside the shop.
Prisoner's Defence. I was with another boy; he snatched the stockings; I ran after him; - they hung quite outside the door.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18261207-266]
269. HENRY GUPPY and JAMES CROKER were indicted for stealing, on the 9th of November, 1 handkerchief, value 3s., the goods of Roger Hunt, from his person.
ROGER HUNT. I am a clerk in the office of the Bank Solicitor. I was on Ludgate-hill on the 9th of November; there was no procession passing then; my handkerchief was in my left-hand coat-pocket; I heard a sudden scuffle behind me; I turned round, and saw the officer holding Croker by the collar: he had my handkerchief in his hand; I then missed it from my pocket: - I saw Guppy at a short distance - he stood for a moment, and then ran down the hill.
JOSEPH PINE. I am a Bow-street patrol. I was coming up Ludgate-hill - I saw the two prisoners and another following two gentlemen very closely; I then saw Guppy take Mr. Hunt's handkerchief and give it to Croker. I ran and took Guppy.
ROBERT TYRRELL. I was with Pine. I saw Guppy take the handkerchief, and give it to Croker, whom I seized as he was putting it under his coat - he dropped it - and I took it up.
GUPPY - GUILTY. Aged 17.
CROKER - GUILTY. Aged 22.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18261207-267]
270. THOMAS JOHNSON was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of December, one 10l. Bank note, the property of George Jaques.
GEORGE JAQUES. I am an attorney's-clerk - I live in Friar-street, Blackfriar's-road. I was going up Ludgate-hill on the 8th of December, a little before ten o'clock; the prisoner overtook me, and when I had reached the corner of Creed-lane, some person walking before us dropped a pin-case - and then the prisoner (or some person very much like him) stooped and said he had found something, and he would have a bit of fun with the person who dropped it - he thought he was a tailor, and he would bother him a bit by taking the needles out and putting in a pin; I advised him to do nothing of the sort, but to return it to the person with the needles in it, but he persisted in putting his plan into execution; I did not see what he did with it, but he called to the person before, and asked if he had lost any thing; he said, Yes - he had lost a needle-case; he said, "What is there in it?" the man said, "There is a needle;" the prisoner said, "Are you sure there was a needle in it?" the man said, Yes; the prisoner said, "I will bet you three cups of coffee there is not;" he said he would bet him a 5l. note of it; the prisoner said, "No, I will not bet 5l., I will bet you three cups of coffee;" the other said, "No, I will not bet so small a sum as that - I will bet 5l.; and he said hebelieved the prisoner had no money in his pocket to bet with; I advised the man not to bet his 5l. with the prisoner, as I thought the prisoner had changed it - the man got very violent, because I interfered - and said he believed I had no money in my pocket; I was a little off my guard, and was induced to bet the 5l. wager; we stopped at the end of Creed-lane, and then the wager was made; I said I would bet 5l. with the man who dropped it, that there was not a needle in the case; I covered it with a 10l. note, and the money was put into the hand of the prisoner - I did it under an impression that the prisoner had taken it out, and with a view of returning him the 5l. note- another person then came up, and the money was put into his hands - the needle-case was produced, and a needle and a pin were found in it; the person who dropped it opened it, and there was a needle and pin in it - they ran away.
Cross-examined by Mr. PHILLIPS. Q. You were pretty sure you should win? A. Yes - I did not intend to pocket his 5l.; I should not have kept it if I had won. I swear that - I am clerk to Mr. Downes - it was his note; I cannot swear the prisoner is the man.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18261207-268]
271. JOHN STRAHAN was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of November, 1 handkerchief, value 8d., and 3lbs. weight of velvet strippings, value 1s., the goods of Daniel Smith, from the person of Daniel Shopperson .
The prosecutor did not appear. NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18261207-269]
First London Jury - Before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
272. EDWARD ROBINSON was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of December, 1 handkerchief, value 6d., the goods of Charles Cunningham, from his person.
CHARLES CUNNINGHAM. I am a mercer, and live in Bernard-street, Russell-square. On the 7th of December, I was in Lothbury, about five o'clock in the evening, and had a handkerchief in my coat pocket; I felt some one turn up my pocket; I turned round and saw the prisoner very near me: he passed me as I put my hand at my pocket: I examined my hat to see if it was there: - I then went and said to the prisoner, "Have you by chance found my handkerchief?" I saw it in his hand - he said Yes. I said I know where you found it, and detained him.
SAMUEL TURNER. I am a constable. I took him in charge, with the handkerchief - he said he had picked it up, but it was a rainy dirty evening, and the handkerchief was quite clean.
Prisoner's Defence. I saw it laying in a door-way - a gentleman saw me pick it up, but I have not had time to send for him.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18261207-270]
273. JOHN MANLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of November, 1 wooden-box, value 1s. 6d.; 1 book, value 1s.; 34 pots of tomato-paste, value 3l., and 4 ozs. of gum-paste ornaments, value 2s., the goods of Thomas Russell, and others.
JOHN CHASE. I am book-keeper to T. Russell and others, who are carriers, and live in Friday-street, Cheapside. On the 18th of November the prisoner came into the office, and asked if the Colchester waggon went from there - I said, No; and as he went out I saw him take a box - I went into the yard, and asked the porters if he had brought one in - they said, No; I called to him; he took no notice - I sent the porters after him; they brought him back, and he said he had taken the wrong box, and it was heavier than the one he brought - I told him to put it down where he took it from, and take the one he had brought; he could not find that; he was a stranger; the one he took had come from Plymouth, and contained this property.
JOHN ROBINSON. I am a porter at this inn. I saw the prisoner come up the yard; he had no box; he came out of the warehouse with one; Chase sent me after him, and he said he had made a mistake; he returned with me, and put it down.
STEPHEN SIMPSON. I am a porter at this inn. I met the prisoner going into the warehouse; he had no box then.
JOHN WILLIS. I am an officer, and took him in charge.
ROBERT ORTON. I packed up this box at Plymouth; it contains the articles stated in the indictment.
The prisoner pleaded distress, and received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18261207-271]
274. THOMAS COURTNEY LLOYD was indicted for perjury.
MR. LAW (on behalf of the Prosecution) declined offering any evidence.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18261207-272]
275. ELLEN McDONALD was indicted for a misdemeanor.
MR. LAW conducted the prosecution.
MARK LEVI. I live in Swan-court, Petticoat-lane, and sell fruit. On the 9th of November, about half-past three o'clock, the prisoner asked the price of a few nuts, which I had in my basket; I said 1d. - she asked if I could change a half-crown, which she gave me; I gave her four sixpences and 5d.; a cart was passing, and I lost sight of her in a moment - I looked at the half-crown, and found it was bad - I have kept it separate from others - I saw her in the custody of Levison in half an hour; she handed my change to another woman; and when I sounded the half-crown, I missed them both; I had seen her several times.
JACOB ARDETI (through an interpreter). I live in Petticoat-lane. About four weeks ago, as I stood in Harrow-alley, Petticoat-lane, the prisoner asked to buy a pair of slippers for 3d., and gave me a bad shilling, which I returned to her - I saw Levison there - he took the shilling from her.
ABRAHAM LEVISON. I saw the prisoner with Ardeti; I took the shilling from her, and delivered it with her to Forrester; I saw her throw something among the people; I could not find it.
JOHN FORRESTER. I am a constable, and received her in charge with the shilling; she had not a half-penny about her.
JOHN FIELD. I am Inspector of money; this half-crown and shilling are both counterfeits.
Prisoner's Defence. I took the shilling in the lane.
GUILTY. Of uttering the half-crown only.
Confined Six Months, and to find Sureties.
[trialAccount t18261207-273]
276. REBECCA CARLTON was indicted for a misdemeanor.
JOHN BASTABLE. I am a butcher, and live in Long-lane. On the 11th of November, the prisoner came into my shop, about four o'clock, and bought a sheep's heart, which came to 3d., and paid me a bad shilling - I gave her a 6d. and 3d.; on her going out, I sounded the shilling - my son-in-law said it was bad, and fetched her back; my daughter searched her - I gave the shilling to Todd.
THOMAS TODD. I am Bastable's son-in-law - I fetched the prisoner back; my wife searched her; she resisted a great deal - my wife found she had a pocket behind, between her shoulders; she called for help, and the officer went in - I took the shilling, and gave it to Pye.
EDWARD JONES. I am a milkman - the prisoner was brought back to the shop by Todd; he showed me the shilling; she was taken into the back-parlour.
THOMAS PYE. I am a constable. I saw her searched, and the pocket found; Jones gave me the shilling - fifteen counterfeit shillings were in the coat-pocket; and in her basket were several odd articles of provisions, all in small quantities.
JOHN FIELD. This shilling and these fifteen are all counterfeit, and from the same die.
Prisoner. I found the packet in Long-lane - I was so rejoiced, I took out 1s., and went to buy a heart.
GUILTY. Aged 12.
Confined One Year, and find Sureties for Two Years.
[trialAccount t18261207-274]
277. MARY GARRON was indicted for fraud.
ELIZABETH SAUNDERS. I am barmaid to W. Shaw Hill, who keeps a wine-vaults in Fore-street. On the 31st of October, about three o'clock in the afternoon, the prisoner came there, and said she wanted a bottle of gin for Mr. Johnson - she was formerly in the habit of coming for Mr. Johnson, of Wood-street, and I let her have it; she never had any before, without paying for it.
SAMUEL JOHNSON. The prisoner was my housemaid; I have sent her to Hill's for wine, but not spirits - she left me on the 28th of October; I did not send her on the 31st- she lived about fifteen months with me. I believe her husband has misled her.
JOSEPH HORTON. I am an officer, and took her in charge.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Strongly Recommended to Mercy.
Confined One Month.
[supplementaryMaterial o18261207-1]
The KING against DUNN and SMITH.
On the second day of the Sessions, Mr. JUSTICE PARK delivered the decision of the Twelve Judges upon the above Case, which confirmed the conviction. Dunn has accordingly received sentence of Death - and Smith of Transportation for Fourteen Years. The particulars of the above decision shall appear in the next Report.
